## Document 156: AI“世纪IPO之战”：OpenAI奋起直追，与Anthropic差距正在缩小

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“世纪IPO之战”：OpenAI奋起直追，与Anthropic差距正在缩小.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

曾独占企业市场四分之三份额的 Anthropic ，正遭遇 OpenAI 的强⼒反扑。 GPT-5.6 系列以
激进降价撕开缺⼝， Fable 5 数据留存政策则加速了客⼾出⾛。两家万亿估值的 AI 巨头均在
筹备 IPO ，⽽企业客⼾ " 哪家便宜⽤哪家 " 的冷酷逻辑，正让这场价格战愈演愈烈。
摘要：
今 年 以 来 ， A I ⼤ 模 型 领 域 的 商 业 竞 争 格 局 正 在 发 ⽣ 显 著 逆 转 。 曾 ⼀ 度 主 导 企 业 市 场 的
Anthropic ，正⾯临 OpenAI 的强⼒追赶 —— ⽽这场⻆⼒的终极舞台，是两家公司即将到来的
IPO 。
据 OpenRouter 最新数据，在同时使⽤ Anthropic 和 OpenAI 产品的约 12 万家企业中，两家公司
的⽀出占⽐在 9 ⽉已⼤致持平。⽽在今年年初， Anthropic ⼀家独占这⼀群体总⽀出的四分之
三。
这⼀逆转的核⼼驱动⼒，是 OpenAI 今年夏季推出的 GPT-5.6 系列模型 —— 该系列以成本效率为

核⼼设计理念，并在发布后随即⼤幅降价。与此同时， Anthropic 在其旗舰模型 Fable 5 上设置的
数据留存政策，也令部分企业客⼾望⽽却步。
两家公司均⾯临资本⽀出急剧攀升的压⼒，且均在筹备 IPO ： Anthropic 最早可能于今年 11 ⽉上
市， OpenAI 则预计明年跟进。华尔街正密切审视两家公司能否以可持续的商业模式和营收增
⻓，⽀撑起各⾃逾万亿美元的估值。
GPT-5.6 ： OpenAI 的价格战反攻
OpenAI 的反攻，以今年 6 ⽉ GPT-5.6 系列的发布为标志性节点。该系列包含 Sol 、 Terra 和 Luna 三
款模型，覆盖不同能⼒层级，为企业客⼾提供了更灵活的成本选择。发布后不久， OpenAI 进⼀
步将 GPT-5.6 Luna 的价格下调 80% ， GPT-5.6 Terra 下调 20% 。
据华尔街⽇报报道，多位创始⼈和⾏业分析师将 GPT-5.6 的发布视为市场格局的转折点。
OpenRouter 洞察负责⼈ Peter Walker 表⽰： "GPT-5.6 为 OpenAI 开辟了⼀条新赛道，⽽

Anthropic 的模型系列⽬前并不具备这样的对应产品。 "
软件开发平台 Retool 的创始⼈兼 CEO David Hsu 表⽰，年初他的公司还在 OpenAI 和 Anthropic 之
间来回切换，但 GPT-5.6 的发布是 Retool 转向主要使⽤ OpenAI 模型的 " 主要催化剂 " 。 " 我们希望
找到最便宜的模型，因为模型越便宜，我们的收⼊就越⾼， "Hsu 说。他估计，⽬前使⽤ OpenAI
模型的成本⽐ Anthropic 低约 20% 。
Fable 5 数据政策： Anthropic 的隐患
OpenAI 的价格攻势之外， Anthropic ⾃⾝的政策失误也加速了部分客⼾的流失。 Anthropic 在今
年 6 ⽉初发布 Fable 5 时宣布，将出于信任与安全⽬的，默认保留⽤⼾数据 30 天。这⼀政策对涉
及敏感数据的⾏业构成合规障碍。
David Hsu 表⽰， Retool ⼏乎所有合同的默认条款都要求数据即时删除， Fable 5 的数据留存机

制使其⽆法满⾜这⼀要求，因此公司从未使⽤该模型。 Anthropic 此后尝试通过向部分客⼾提供
数据管控权限来弥补这⼀问题，但损失已经造成。
企业客⼾：成本压⼒重塑选择逻辑
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

在更宏观的层⾯，企业客⼾的决策逻辑正在发⽣结构性转变。随着越来越多员⼯尝试使⽤ AI ⼯
具，企业⾯临的成本压⼒持续上升，并逐渐意识到并⾮所有任务都需要最顶尖的模型。部分企
业开始将中国低成本开源模型纳⼊其多模型使⽤策略。
AI 销售平台初创公司 Reevo 的联合创始⼈兼 CEO David Zhu 表⽰，其公司正将 AI 使⽤从
Anthropic 转向 OpenAI ，原因有⼆：成本考量，以及降低对单⼀模型供应商的依赖。 " 与某⼀家
模型供应商深度绑定的蜜⽉期已经结束，"Zhu 说，但他也坦⾔偏好随时可能再度转变， " 事情变
化太快了。 "
⾦融科技公司 Ramp 的经济学家 Ara Kharazian 表⽰，他在 9 ⽉中旬观察了雇主旗下 7 万名客⼾的

数据，发现企业在 OpenAI 模型上的⽀出⾃去年 12 ⽉以来⾸次超过 Anthropic 。不过这⼀领先优势
转瞬即逝 —— 到当周末， Anthropic 以微弱优势重新夺回⾸位。
Anthropic ：市场热度犹存，新模型反击
尽管市场份额承压， Anthropic 在开发者社群中的号召⼒依然不容⼩觑。本周三上午，旧⾦⼭市
中⼼⼀处仓库外，⼤批⼈群排起⻓⻰，等待进⼊ Anthropic 主办的 "Claude Founder House" 活
动，部分⼈提前⼀⼩时到场。前⼀天，有⼈等候三⼩时后仍因场地爆满被拒之⻔外，现场盛况
在社交媒体上被⽐作科切拉⾳乐节的排队景象。
视频游戏初创公司 ReadyM 联合创始⼈ Michael Szklarski 表⽰，其公司通常需要最前沿的模型，
倾向于使⽤ Anthropic 的 Fable 5.1 ，但他希望与 Anthropic ⼯程师探讨如何进⼀步降低使⽤成
本。
在竞争压⼒下， Anthropic 于 9 ⽉下旬开始发布新⼀代 Claude 5.5 系列模型，主打更低成本与更⾼

效率。 AI 领域的领先优势向来转瞬即逝，这场争夺企业客⼾的价格战，远未到尘埃落定之时。

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.

Thought of the Week—The Beauty of September: Call it an eventful first 13 trading

days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per barrel, diesel prices surpassed
$6
per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield breached 5%, and
the Federal Reserve (Fed) joined the European Central Bank (ECB) and the Bank of Japan
(BoJ) in hiking rates for the
first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has
shed 1.4% since the start of the month. It’s not unexpected that September would be
choppy for U.S. Equities, especially in a midterm election year. The good news is that

this has historically been an attractive entry point. Since 1950, the S&P 500 has never
been
negative in the six or twelve months post-midterm election, boasting average
returns of 13.8% and 15.8%, respectively. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election. A correction amid a strong fundamental economic backdrop
would be a
potential buying opportunity in our view.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.

The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

4 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

6 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Europe's construction sector is showing signs of stabilising, but growth remains

Low growth returns in 2026
We expect EU construction output to grow by a marginal 0.5% in

2026, following a 1.8% decline in 2024 and 2.3% growth in 2025.
More broadly, EU GDP has outpaced construction output in recent
years, showing that the construction sector has lagged the broader
economy.
The positive signals seen in early 2025 have since weakened. By
September, the EU construction conﬁdence indicator had stopped
improving and had fallen below its level a year earlier. Nevertheless,

The building sector has declined, while infrastructure has grown
The building sector, comprising residential and non-residential

construction costs, combined with economic uncertainty, are
making households and businesses more cautious about investing
in new property. By contrast, some segments stand out more

### Page 3

Source: Eurostat, *forecasts ING Research
Following ﬁve consecutive years of contraction, Germany’s
construction sector is expected to grow again in 2026. Europe’s
largest construction market shrank by more than 10% between
2020 and 2025. We expect a modest recovery this year, with
growth gradually gaining momentum in 2027, supported by
housing and infrastructure.
In 2025, France’s construction output fell by 3.5%, followed by
quarter-on-quarter declines of 1.2% and 0.9% in the ﬁrst two

quarters of 2026. French sector conﬁdence weakened further, while
housing permits in August remained below their level a year earlier.
Combined with sluggish GDP growth, this points to a 2.5%
contraction in 2026.
Spain’s construction sector is still recovering. After losing more than
25% of its output between 2019 and 2022, the market began to
rebound in 2023. We forecast growth of 3% in 2026. Building
permits have risen sharply in recent years, although momentum is

easing. Contractor conﬁdence is easing from a very high level in
recent months. Even so, robust GDP growth should support
construction this year and next.
For the Netherlands, we expect construction output to stabilise in
2026 after a weak ﬁrst half, followed by modest growth in 2027.

### Page 4

Housing construction should gradually recover as the recent rise in
building permits feeds through into starts and completions,
although projects are often delayed and the recovery will remain
limited. Infrastructure remains an important driver.
Poland’s construction sector had a volatile start to 2026, with
output falling by 6.8% in the ﬁrst quarter due to the cold winter
before rebounding by 8% in the second. Building permits are rising
slightly, conﬁdence remains stable and robust GDP growth of 3.4%

should support demand. The resumption of EU funding is also
expected to support infrastructure investment. Nevertheless, we
expect construction output to remain ﬂat in 2026, largely because
of the weak ﬁrst quarter, followed by modest growth of 2% in 2027.
Input prices are marginally increasing
Higher energy prices are pushing up production costs for energy-
intensive materials such as concrete, cement and bricks. Even so,
material prices are generally rising only gradually. In September,

13% of EU producers planned to raise selling prices over the next
three months, compared with 11% in January, before the Iran war.
Passing higher input costs through the value chain is also
becoming harder. Governments, non-residential investors and
homebuyers already face higher interest rates, making further cost
increases more diﬃcult to absorb.
Contract terms may also pose a risk for contractors. Public price-
adjustment schemes are often too limited to absorb shocks, while

countries without them, such as Spain, face greater exposure.
Private contracts oﬀer even less protection, increasing the risk of
disputes, delays and halted projects.

### Page 5

Source: Eurostat, ING Research
Low water levels are delaying deliveries
Low water levels are creating upward cost pressure on building
materials. Many raw materials used in building materials, such as
sand, gravel and chemicals, are transported by inland waterways.
When river levels are low, barges can carry less cargo, which
increases transport costs and delays deliveries in the construction
supply chain. The impact has remained limited but is slowly

increasing. In September, 8% of EU construction companies
reported production constraints due to material shortages. This is
above the 5.7% in January and the 6.4% recorded during the low-
water period in September 2018.
Proﬁt conﬁdence rebounds, but energy risks return
Higher energy prices and the prospect of rising building-material
costs initially alarmed construction companies, which feared a
sharp hit to proﬁt margins in the ﬁrst quarter of 2026. As the Iran

war continued, however, gas prices rose less dramatically than
during the 2022 energy crisis. Conﬁdence recovered, and second-
quarter proﬁt expectations exceeded pre-war levels. This rebound
may prove excessive, as energy prices have since begun rising
again.

### Page 6

Source: RICS, ING Research
Residential growth stalls
Between summer 2024 and the end of 2025, the number of
building permits issued in the EU rose by approximately 15%. Since
then, however, permit issuance has broadly stabilised, suggesting
that momentum has faded in recent months. Several factors may
explain this development:
Higher interest rates make homes less aﬀordable, particularly
new builds, because buyers often have to ﬁnance payments
during construction.

The conﬂict in the Middle East has heightened geopolitical
uncertainty. This may make buyers more reluctant to purchase
a home, especially a new build, because completion can take
up to two years. The longer waiting period increases
uncertainty compared with buying an existing home.
Higher energy prices have increased the cost of energy-
intensive building materials, although the rise in overall
construction costs has so far remained modest.
One possible oﬀset is that buyers may prefer new homes because

they are generally more energy-eﬃcient than existing properties.
However, the stabilisation in permit issuance suggests that this
advantage is not strong enough to drive further growth.
The number of permits issued for new houses is an important
indicator of future volumes in the residential sector. Typically, it
takes between one and two years from the granting of a permit to
the completion of a new home.

### Page 7

Recovery of issued building permits slows
EU Building permits new dwellings, SA (index 2021=100
Germany follows the broader European recovery after a particularly
sharp fall in housing permits in 2021 and 2022. The government’s
‘housing construction turbo’ (Bau-Turbo) aims to speed up
planning and approvals in high-demand areas. Its impact, however,
will depend on local authorities, which may hesitate over loss of
control and legal uncertainty.
The permit data for France at the beginning of the year looked

promising. However, the second quarter was disappointing. France
expanded its zero-interest mortgage scheme (PTZ: Prêt à Taux Zéro)
for new-build homes in April 2025 to support ﬁrst-time buyers and
boost housing construction, but the building permit data suggests
the policy has yet to gain traction.
Dutch permits for new homes surged in the second quarter of 2026.
Statistics Netherlands has since paused publication of recent data,
saying the increase appears implausibly large and may reﬂect

legislative changes related to grid congestion. Limited grid capacity
continues to delay new connections and construction projects,
while nitrogen restrictions cause further setbacks. The
government's plan to reduce nitrogen emissions of peak polluters
and make way for other business activities is now under
parliamentary review.
Spanish housing permits nearly tripled between 2020 and 2024,
largely reﬂecting a rebound from the exceptionally low pre-

### Page 8

pandemic era base and a recovery in housing demand. Growth now
appears to have peaked, with permits falling by almost 2% in the
second quarter of 2026. The government’s PERTE programme, co-
ﬁnanced through European recovery funds, aims to speed up and
reduce the cost of housing construction through prefabrication and
modular methods, but its impact will take time.
Business case still diﬃcult
Housing shortages remain severe in many European cities, but new

housing projects are more diﬃcult to make commercially viable in
some countries than in others. Although permit issuance has
increased in many markets, construction costs remain high.
Builders and developers often cannot fully pass these costs on to
buyers, especially where existing homes are still cheaper than a few
years ago. As buyers can choose between new and existing homes,
relatively higher new-build prices are likely to push many towards
existing properties.

This is particularly true in France, Germany and Austria, where
existing-home prices have risen less than the European average. In
France, they remain even below their levels three years ago.
Builders and developers therefore have very limited (or no) scope to
pass on higher material costs, leaving many projects unproﬁtable
at current market prices. As a result, they are postponing
construction until conditions improve.

Infrastructure growth continues
EU infrastructure output grew by 2.2% in 2025. Growth slowed in
the ﬁrst seven months of 2026 to around 0.4% year-on-year. A
relatively cold, wet winter across many European regions
temporarily disrupted activity at infrastructure sites, though the
sector recovered some of the lost ground in the following months.
Highest growth in energy works
Infrastructure subsectors EU-19, Index 2021=100

Source: Euroconstruct, ING Research
The EU energy construction sector has been growing since 2022,
driven by investments in renewable energy and electricity grid
expansion. Growth is expected to remain strong as many countries
in the EU continue to upgrade power infrastructure to support the
energy transition and rising electricity demand.

Railway construction is showing strong growth. Across the EU,
activity is being supported by both network renewals and major

cross-border projects. Examples include Rail Baltica, which will
connect the Baltic States with Poland, the Lyon–Turin rail link
between France and Italy and high-speed rail investment in Spain,
Portugal and Italy. Together, these projects illustrate the EU’s drive
to expand rail capacity.
Telecommunications construction has grown modestly in recent
years, supported by ﬁbre-optic expansion and continued
investment in 5G networks across the EU. Although coverage is

improving, progress varies widely between member states and
signiﬁcant investment is still needed to extend high-capacity
networks, particularly in rural and other lagging areas.
Roadworks, which account for around 30% of EU infrastructure
output, remain under pressure as many local and regional
authorities face budget constraints. Across the EU, public funding is
competing with other investment and spending priorities, limiting
the resources available for road construction and maintenance.

Overall, Europe’s construction recovery will remain slow and uneven.
Infrastructure investment and a gradual German recovery will
provide support, but high ﬁnancing costs, weak housing economics
and geopolitical risks will limit momentum. For now, infrastructure
will do most of the heavy lifting within the sector.

## Document 259: German Defense_ An Ecosystem in Transformation (2026-09-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/German Defense_ An Ecosystem in Transformation (2026-09-25).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-09-25
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

German Defense:  
An Ecosystem in Transformation

1 
Authors 
Michael Kuhn 
Research Analyst

Tim Rokossa 
Head of European Equity Research

Christoph Laskawi 
Research Analyst

Christophe Menard 
Research Analyst

Sriram Krishnan, CFA 
Research Analyst

September 25, 2026 
This is the first section of a report available to clients via our Deutsche Bank 
Research site here. 
Launching Private Company Analysis with the German Defense Sector 
Traditional analysis that focused on public companies overlooked a critical 
market segment: the many important competitors that are privately owned, 
typically by families or private equity firms. To address this, we are launching 
dedicated analysis of Europe’s private-market giants, starting with Germany, 
where a traditionally dominant private sector means large firms outnumber 
public ones almost two to one. We begin with the German defense sector, where 
understanding the private ecosystem is essential: it is the source of critical 
innovation, the solution to supply-chain issues, and the venue for a future wave 
of M&A, consolidation, and IPOs. 
From Incremental Modernization to Industrial Scaling 
Germany's defense landscape is undergoing its most significant transformation 
in decades, shifting from a paradigm of 'incremental modernization' to 'industrial 
scaling' . Triggered by the Zeitenwende in 2022, the new era is underpinned by 
substantial financial commitments, including a €100bn special fund and 
constitutional reforms enabling sustained long-term spending. The objective is 
no longer merely to fill discrete capability gaps but to execute a full 'ecosystem 
build-out' that addresses structural choke points and enhances industrial 
capacity. This transformation is creating a more resilient and scalable German 
Defense Industrial Base (DIB), prepared to meet surging domestic and European 
demand. 
Technology, Integration, and the Rise of New Players 
This strategic pivot is also reshaping the industrial and technological 
environment. EU initiatives (EDF , EDIRPA, ASAP) are fostering joint funding and 
integrated supply chains, while the market is experiencing a 'next wave' of 
technological change, with a clear shift in value towards drones, software-
defined systems, electronic warfare, and AI-driven capabilities. These dynamics 
force industry consolidation but also create openings for agile start-ups, which 
are vital for innovation, as speed and software become paramount. This report 
provides an in-depth primer on this evolving sector. We identify Germany’s 
industrial strengths and technology gaps, analyze the key private players poised 
to benefit, and examine the structural themes shaping the market - from funding 
dynamics and procurement reforms to technology roadmaps.

### Page 2

25 September 2026
Aerospace & Defence
Defense
Page 2 Deutsche Bank AG
Half the Industry is Invisible
We are Changing that.
Equity research has always looked at the listed half of the economy. That half is
shrinking. Companies stay private for longer, the largest capital pools are now
private, and in Germany the listed universe was never the industry to begin with.
There are roughly 120 listed German companies with sales above €1bn - and
roughly 200 unlisted ones.  In our home market, large private companies

outnumber public ones almost two to one.
That gap is not a curiosity. It is an analytical problem.
If a sector's innovation, its supply-chain frictions, its pricing behavior and its
eventual consolidation sit largely outside the listed universe, then covering only the
listed names means covering only part of the argument. With this report we start
private-market giants, beginning in Germany.
Why we are starting with German defense
Defense is the sharpest version of the problem we are trying to solve. Germany is

in the middle of its largest rearmament cycle since reunification, but much of the
capability being bought is not primarily produced by the listed defense groups. It is
produced by an ecosystem: family-owned system houses that have supplied the
Bundeswehr for decades, venture-capital-backed companies that did not exist
three years ago, and the tier-2 and tier-3 suppliers who sit on the actual constraints
- energetics, seekers, qualified electronics, test capacity, cleared labor. That

ecosystem is where the innovation happens, where the supply chain either breaks
Understanding it is not a side-project to covering Rheinmetall, Hensoldt or Renk. It
is a prerequisite.
Executive Summary - Eight Points Carry this Report
1.  The spending shift is structural, not cyclical. The €100bn special fund was
the headline; the 2025 constitutional reform exempting defense spending
above 1% of GDP from the debt brake was the substance. Government

budgets now foresee more than €150bn of annual defense spending by
2029, against roughly €52bn of core budget in 2024. NATO's 2025 pledge
of 3.5% of GDP on core defense by 2035 puts a floor under the back-end.
2.  Budgets are not revenue. Between an approved budget and a delivered
capability sit parliamentary approval for anything above €25m, tender
design, contracting, qualification, test capacity and acceptance. The
February 2026 procurement acceleration reform helps, but the conversion

chain - not demand - sets the delivery slope.
3.  Value is moving to software, autonomy and effects.  Drones, software-
defined systems, electronic warfare and AI-enabled decision layers are
taking share from platform-centric spend. That favors companies whose
iteration cycle is measured in weeks, and it is the clearest reason private
and venture-capital-backed firms now sit inside mainstream force design.
4.  Production is deterrence - and production is where Germany is short.

Scaling breaks at specific points: energetics permits, seekers and
guidance electronics, qualification burden, specialized labor, and limited
or scales.
closing that gap. We are launching dedicated, ongoing analysis of Europe's

### Page 3

25 September 2026
Aerospace & Defence
Defense
Deutsche Bank AG Page 3
test windows. Almost every one of those nodes is occupied, at least in part,
by a private company.
5.  The private ecosystem is now procurement-relevant, not just innovation-
relevant. The February 2026 Bundeswehr loitering-munitions framework,
awarded to Helsing and STARK from an initial package of roughly €540m,
is the clearest proof point. Sovereign buyers are contracting with

companies two to five years old.
6.  Scale still sits with the incumbents. Diehl generated €5.4bn of sales and
€703m of EBIT in FY2025, with Defence at roughly €2.3bn; Rohde &
Schwarz passed €3bn of revenue in FY2024/25 with no bank debt. These
are not start-ups with a defense angle - they are among Europe's more
significant defense industrial assets, and they happen to be family-owned.
Regulation is the binding constraint, in both directions. Export licensing,

foreign investment screening, NATO interoperability and compliance
intensity limit scaling and exits. But policy also accelerates - fast-track
procurement, EU joint programs, state-backed vehicles for defense tech.
Why defense, in numbers
The money. Germany's core defense budget has moved from roughly €47bn in
2021 to a projected path above €150bn by 2029, with the €100bn special fund
bridging the middle of that ramp. The 2025 debt-brake reform matters more than

the fund itself: by exempting defense spending above 1% of GDP, it converts a one-
off program into a sustained baseline. Procurement approvals have already
responded: 97 major defense projects approved in 2024 against 55 in 2023, with
over €58bn allocated.

The conversion chain.  What is in doubt is throughput. Contracts above €25m
require binding Bundestag budget-committee approval, and tendering, supplier
orders, integration, testing and acceptance each add time. Europe's fragmentation
compounds it: European states operate around 179 different major weapon
systems against 33 in the US, and 19 different main battle tanks against one.

Between 2020 and 2024, European arms imports rose 155%, and European NATO
members sourced 64% of those imports from the US, up from 52% in 2015-19.
Whether 'buy European' becomes policy rather than rhetoric is the single largest
swing factor for the domestic industrial base.
Figure 2: Budget to Output: The Conversion Chain
Source : Deutsche Bank research
Where the private sector sits. The constrained nodes are consistent across almost
every program: energetics capacity, defense-qualified electronics, test and

certification, and cleared labor. Private companies are disproportionately present in
exactly those nodes. Diehl's acquisition of Dynamit Nobel was, in substance, the
acquisition of explosives-related operating authorizations it describes as unique in
Germany. STARK's dependence on TDW for German-manufactured warheads is
the same story one tier down. Rohde & Schwarz occupies the secure-
communications and SIGINT/EW layer no platform program can route around.

Figure 3: German Defense Industrial Value Chain
Source : Deutsche Bank research

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5
Where the new money is going. Germany attracted close to €1bn of defense and
security venture-capital funding in 2025, second only to the US. The deal mix since
2021: unmanned systems (34 deals), surveillance and space (24), other defense
tech (24), electronic warfare (10) and cyber (8). Helsing raised $1.8bn at an $18bn
valuation in July 2026; STARK Defence raised €500m in June 2026 above €3.5bn.

Capital of that scale changes the industrial map.

The five debates - and where we go next
1.  Can Germany convert budget into capability fast enough?  Demand is
settled; throughput is not. Watch acceptance capacity, qualification

timelines and framework design rather than headline budgets.
2.  Do the newcomers industrialize, or stall at the factory door?  Helsing,
STARK Defence, ARX Robotics and Quantum Systems are all making the
same transition from software and prototypes into serial hardware. That is
where defense start-ups historically fail. The next 18 months are the test.
3.  Do incumbents get disrupted, or absorb? The likelier outcome is a denser
partner network - Diehl with Hensoldt and Airbus, Helsing with Saab and

Kongsberg, ARX Robotics with RENK and Daimler Truck. Partnership maps
are becoming a better guide to competitive position than product catalogs.
4.  Does 'buy European' become real? A conscious substitution of even part
of the 64% US import share would be a structural repricing of the European
industrial base. Fragmentation and interoperability standards cut against
it.
5.  Who is financed, and how? Rising capital intensity, thin private disclosure,

constrained exit routes under foreign-investment screening, and uneven
ESG treatment will all influence the financing and consolidation wave.

### Page 6

25 September 2026
Aerospace & Defence
Defense
Page 6 Deutsche Bank AG
The Argument in Five Parts
1. The Strategic Landscape: Russia's 2022 invasion of Ukraine, combined with the
distancing of the US as Europe's protector, represents a turning point for the safety
of Europe and its defense industry. The shift in policy is creating immense demand
across Europe for arms in the decades to come, leading to significant M&A and
investing opportunities. German companies are uniquely suited to serve the rising

demand due to their immense technical know-how and their already-existing
industrial sector. Combined with targeted funding programs, these developments
both stimulate existing companies and incentivize start-ups that push the technical
frontier forward.
2. New Wars Change the Old Battlefield: Recent wars in Ukraine and the Middle
East have reshaped modern warfare, shifting capital from traditional contractors to
start-ups that can keep pace with rapid innovation cycles. Key trends defining this

new era include: unmanned systems creating a transparent battlefield that
demands adaptability; huge munitions consumption proving that production itself
is deterrence; the battlespace expanding into commercial space and cyberspace;
and societal resilience against disinformation becoming a vital security element.
This all occurs amid a new great power competition, heightening the risk of multi-
theater wars and the use of nuclear threats to back conventional aggression.

3. How to Gain Exposure – Investment Opportunities in Private Gems:  Rapid
innovation in modern warfare is creating significant investment opportunities in
Germany's defense sector, as start-ups outperform slow, traditional procurement.
This operational shift is fueled by a long-term surge in European defense spending
targeted at R&D and equipment. Investors can gain exposure through key 'private
gems', from established firms like Diehl Group and Rohde & Schwarz to high-

growth start-ups like Helsing (AI) and Quantum Systems (drones). These start-ups
leverage a mix of venture capital and government grants with innovative business
models, making the German private defense market a compelling investment area.
4. Trend – Europe Buys European & Europe Buys between Middle Powers: Recent
energy and security shocks have shattered Europe's post-Cold War assumptions,
creating an urgent push for strategic self-reliance amid uncertainty over the long-

term US security guarantee. This pivot is evident in new security partnerships, such
as Germany deepening defense ties in the Indo-Pacific (Japan) and North Atlantic
(Canada). Paradoxically, Europe's rearmament has increased its reliance on US
hardware (64% of imports '20-'24) and its defense sector remains highly
fragmented. The core challenge is whether Europe's quest for autonomy will lead
to a 'buy European' policy; doing so would unlock a monumental internal market,

driving consolidation and creating a truly independent defense capability.
5. The Regulatory Landscape & Investment Constraints: The defense industry is
uniquely constrained and regulated, and investors must understand its landscape.
Growth is not driven simply by demand but is filtered through government budgets,
procurement laws, and export controls. This creates risks: budgets don't translate
to immediate revenue, market access can be denied politically, and production

cannot be scaled freely. High compliance costs and national security reviews that
limit exit strategies add further complexity. Successful investment, therefore,
requires mastering these political and regulatory realities, not just identifying
demand.

German Defense: An Ecosystem in Transformation – September 25, 2026

摘要：
Manus ⺟公司蝴蝶效应完成超 5 亿美元新⼀轮融资，估值升⾄约 40 亿美元，创下国内 Agent 原⽣
创企单笔融资最⾼纪录。这笔资⾦将⽀撑公司推进通⽤ Agent 产品扩张，并重返国内市场。
本轮融资由博裕投资、 IDG 资本领投，⽼股东腾讯、红杉中国、真格基⾦继续跟投。据彭博此前
报道，蝴蝶效应计划以 40 亿美元估值融资 5 亿美元，较上⼀轮 20 亿美元估值翻倍。融资消息宣布
的同⽇，公司⾯向海内外公开 17 个岗位招聘，⼯作地点均设于北京。
此次融资落地，距蝴蝶效应 9 ⽉ 1 ⽇正式宣布恢复独⽴运营仅逾⼀个⽉。就在融资宣布前数⽇，
公司发布了 Manus 2.0 及多 Agent 群聊应⽤ Cue ，这是恢复独⽴运营后的⾸次重⼤产品更新。头

部机构集中押注，折射出资本市场对 " 能执⾏、可落地 "Agent 产品的持续关注，也为蝴蝶效应下
⼀阶段的产品与商业化竞争提供了弹药。
恢复独⽴运营后⾸次产品⼤更新
9 ⽉ 29 ⽇，蝴蝶效应发布 Manus 2.0 及个⼈智能体应⽤ Cue 。 Manus 2.0 采⽤⾃研 Agent 框架
Cascade ，项⽬从起步阶段保持轻量化，仅在任务有实际需求时调⽤对应专业能⼒。
内部测试数据显⽰，与此前系统相⽐， Cascade 将 Token 消耗减少 23.2% ，任务完成时间缩短
28.2% ，运⾏成本降低 32% 。Cue ⾯向个⼈⽣活场景，与 Manus 共⽤基础设施，⽀持⼿机和桌⾯
端，具备代接电话、在⽤⼾设定预算内付款、在群聊中分⼯处理事务等功能。
Manus 2.0 与 Cue ⽬前⾯向海外推出，国内产品仍在筹备阶段。公司已宣布正在组建国内产品团
队，并推进与国产模型⼚商及⽣态伙伴的合作。
坚持 “ 通⽤ Agent” 定位，商业价值待证
在外部竞争⽇趋激烈的背景下，蝴蝶效应仍坚持通⽤ Agent 定位。 CEO 肖弘⽤ " 卖电脑 " 类⽐这⼀

路线：同⼀套计算能⼒可承载⼯作、创作与娱乐等不同需求。按照这⼀逻辑， Manus 定位⼯作
与创作场景， Cue 延伸⾄个⼈事务与⽇常协作，两款产品共享基础设施。

2026/9/30Quarterly Market Report
2
日本の金融政策見通し：（井上）
米国の金融政策見通し（井上）
＜メインシナリオ（需要主導のインフレ圧力へ
シフト）：緩やかな利上げ（確率60％）＞
 FRBは、9月の見通しで、経済が力強さを増したと
評価した一方、インフレ率の2％目標への収斂に
時間を要する点を懸念
 FOMCメンバーは2026年中にも う1回の利上げを
見込む一方、2027年中は1回の利上げと現状維
持ないし利下げに2分。この間、市場は合計3回を
超える利上げを織り込み
 2026年中は、既往のエネルギー価格上昇の国内
への波及リスクに対して12月に1回の利上げを行
い、2027年には需要主導のインフレ圧力を抑制す
る目的でもう1回の利上げを行い、政策金利（FF
レートの誘導目標）は4.25～4.5％へ
 その後は、政策金利が明確に引き締め領域に入
るだけに、FRBも経済や物価の反応を見極めるた
め、政策金利を現状維持
＜代替シナリオ②（インフレ圧力が早期に沈

静化）：利上げを停止（同15％）＞
 エネルギー価格の迅速な低下によって、供給要因
による国内物価への波及が迅速に収束
 需要主導のインフレ圧力は残存するが、2026年12
月の利上げによって4％台に達する政策金利によ
り十分引き締め的と判断して現状維持に転換
 資産価格のオーバーシュートとその後の急速な調
整によって、経済の下方リスクがむしろ増大
 基調的インフレのアンカーがポイント
＜メインシナリオ（基調的インフレ率が目標に
収斂）：政策金利の速やかな中立化（確率
60％）＞
 日銀は、9月利上げの際に、経済の底堅さに自信
を示す一方、基調的インフレの上振れリスクを意識
 植田総裁は、基調的インフレを2％近傍に収斂させ
ることを主眼とする「新たな局面」に入ったとの認識
 本年12月にさらに25bpの利上げを行い、年末には
1.50％。2027年3月にも追加利上げで政策金利は
1.75％へ到達
 ただし、政策金利が明確に中立化する中で、経済
活動への負荷が徐々に明確になる可能性

 日銀によるビハインドザカーブの懸念が後退する
中で、長期金利の焦点は財政運営（消費減税の財
源等）や米国の長期金利による影響へ
＜代替シナリオ①（インフレ圧力が想定以上
に継続）：利上げを継続（同25％）＞
 景気が底堅い下で、エネルギー価格の高止まりに
よる国内物価への波及が強まりつつ継続
 インフレ期待を抑制するため、FRBは2027年中に
利上げを継続し、2027年末に5％近傍へ上昇
 経済や物価への影響だけでなく、金融環境のタイ
ト化が非直線的に出現するリスク
＜代替シナリオ②（経済の回復が想定以上に
遅延）：利上げの減速（同10％）＞
 輸出の好調が内需に波及せず、経済活動が停滞
 円安の抑制も必要であるため、日銀はより緩やかな
ペースの利上げにシフト
 日米金利差の拡大による円安圧力が輸入インフレ
→実質購買力低下→一層の内需停滞を招くリスク
＜代替シナリオ①（エネルギー価格の高騰が
想定以上に継続）：金融引き締めの領域へ（同
30％）＞
 エネルギー価格の高止まり、ないし円安の継続に

よって国内物価への波及が想定以上に長く継続
 基調的インフレの上振れを阻止するため、日銀は利
上げを継続し、ターミナルレートは2％台前半に上昇
 経済と物価のトレードオフが深刻化し、政府の経済
政策との調整が困難化

日本経済見通し（宮嶋）
米国経済見通し（渡辺）
＜ポイント＞
 足下では台風や長雨等による悪天候や中東情
勢悪化によるコスト高が逆風の中 、AIブームによ
る米国経済の堅調さ 、実質賃金の緩やかな改善
（上図）等から、景気は底堅く推移
 米国経済の緩やかな回復が続く中 、食料品の消
費減税による押し 上げ効果等から、27～28年度
の景気は回復局面が継続 。29年度は減税効果
剥落を想定し てやや減速も、需給ギャップはプラ
ス圏で推移すると予想（下図）
＜リスク＞
 中東情勢の予想以上の悪化により 、原油高が一

段と進行すればコスト高の逆風が強まるリスク
 高市政権の積極財政による景気上振れ。ただし、
市場不安定化による金利高等の副作用の懸念も
＜ポイント＞
 米国経済は個人消費と 設備投資を牽引役に堅調持
続（上図）。個人消費は株高の資産効果が背景
 金利上昇で住宅市場は悪化。一方、潤沢なキャッ
シュフローを背景にAIデータセンター投資は活況
 足元の株式市場はAI企業の利益成長が主導する
「業績相場」と、金利上昇でPERが下がる「逆金融相
場」が併存。綱引きで株価には停滞感出るも、長期
的には名目GDPに沿って上昇継続の公算（下図）
 大統領2年目の株価は、中間選挙が与党不利と なり
やすく、政策停滞懸念から軟調傾向。3、4年目は大
統領選に向けた政策テコ入れで騰勢が強まる傾向
＜リスク＞
 金利急騰やAIブームの腰折れで株安と景気後退
 イラン紛争の長期化によるスタグフレーション
 インフレ急進とFRBの大幅利上げで景気後退

単位労働コストの抑制が基調物価を押し下げ

（単位労働コスト前年比、％）
（年）
（前年比、％）
基調的な物価
（コアPCEデフレータの景気循環部分、右軸）
単位労働コスト
（３四半期先行、左軸）
日本物価見通し（宮嶋）
米国物価見通し（渡辺）
＜ポイント＞
 関税や中東情勢に伴うエネルギー高によりコア財
物価が上振れ（上図）
 長期インフレ予想は安定しており、賃金・サービス
物価への二次的波及は限定的。エネルギー高が
ホームメード・インフレに発展するリスクは限定的
 労働生産性の上昇が単位労働コストを抑え 、基
調的な物価を抑制する見込み（下図）

 供給要因のインフレは27年前半に剥落へ。ただし、
堅調な米景気がディマンドプル ・イ ンフレを 招き、
27年以降も物価は2％目標に着地しない公算
＜リスク＞
 AI関連の需要増で電子部品や電力の価格を中心
にインフレ上振れ
 AIによる生産性向上のディスインフレ圧力
 イラン紛争早期終結で油価下落、インフレ下振れ
 トランプ大統領がFRBの独立性を脅かし、金融政
策の信認と物価抑制効果が低下する恐れ
 政府債務増大とドル価値の低下に伴う インフレ
＜ポイント＞
 現状の物価は政府の支援策により上値が抑えら
れている格好（上図）。同対応策の終了を想定す
れば、中東情勢悪化によるインフレ圧力により 、
コアCPIは26年度末に3％近傍に加速する公算
 今後の中東情勢の小康状態を想定し 、食料品の
消費減税による下押し効果もあり 、27年度のコア
CPIは減速に向かう と 予想。28年度以降は減税
効果剥落により 再び加速へ。減税効果を除けば 、
物価は2％目標圏内で推移すると予想

 長期インフレ予想は足下 2％程度（下図）。今後
は中東情勢悪化による影響剥落や物価目標達
成への信認から目標圏で推移と予想
＜リスク＞
 原油高や財政悪化懸念、それに伴う 円安が再度
進めば物価上振れリスク大
 食料品の消費減税に関わる政策の詳細が不透
明で、物価への影響も 不確実性が大きい
注：コアCPIは生鮮食品除くベース
インフレ率は2027年以降も2％目標に着地しない公算

市場予想
（左軸）
（期待政策金利、％）
日本の金利見通し（宮嶋）
米国の金利見通し（渡辺）
＜ポイント＞
 日銀はインフレ上振れ防止のため利上げをペー
スアップ 。先行き は、12月・来年3月に利上げ予
想（金融政策の頁参照） 。その後の政策金利は
基調物価の2％前後の推移の下で横ばいを想定
 長期金利は 、利上げペースアップ等から 上昇に
歯止め。ただし 、米金利高や日銀国債保有減少
等から、3％近傍の高水準継続と予想（上図）
 超長期金利は世界的な財政懸念等が逆風も、発

行減額等への期待から小幅に低下へ （下図）
＜リスク＞
 想定以上のイ ンフレ加速と なれば、政策金利が
2％を超え、イールドカーブ上方シフトのリスク
 グローバルな財政懸念や高市政権の日銀利上
げ牽制姿勢が強まれば 、長・超長期ゾーンの金
利上昇が加速（スティープ化）
＜ポイント＞
 長期金利＝期待政策金利（政策金利予想）＋ター
ムプレミアム（国債保有リスク）（上図）
 タームプレミアムの上昇要因は、トランプ政策を巡
る 不確実性から、財政不安や国債の需給悪化懸
念、中間選挙後の不透明感へシフト。AI企業の大
量起債に伴う投資家資本の争奪戦も一因
 期待政策金利が上昇。イ ラ ン 紛 争 に 伴 うコ ス ト プ ッ
シュ・インフレと 堅調な経済指標がFRBを タカ 派化さ
せ、市場の利上げ期待を押し上げる（下図）
＜リスク＞
 イラン紛争早期終結で原油安、金利低下
 インフレや財政悪化、トランプ氏のFRBに対する政
治圧力等による「悪い金利上昇」
 ウォ ーシュFRB議長のフォワードガイダンス曖昧化

で、金融市場のボラティリティが上昇
 FRBの資産圧縮の加速でタームプレミアム急上昇
 金利上昇による景気後退の結果、金利下振れ
米国：長期金利＝期待政策金利＋タームプレミアム
注：期待政策金利とタームプレミアムはNY連銀のACMモデルの推計値

（日米実質金利差、％pt）

（1ドル＝円）
当
社
予
想
ドル円レート
（右軸）日米実質金利差
（左軸）
ドル円の見通し（尾河）
＜ポイント＞
 前四半期から全体的にドル円の長期見通しを
小幅ながら円高方向に修正
 今年の円安トレンドのテーマであった日銀の
「利上げの遅れ（Behind the Curve）」は後退
 日米の利上げは、共にあと2回程度と 予想。日
米の金利差が著し く開く、或いは縮小はせず、

今後1 年程度は155 円を 中心とし た上下10円
程度のボックス相場が続くと 予想
 一方で、今後デジタル赤字がさらに膨らむ可
能性など 、構造的な円安要因は残るため （下
図）、その後は緩やかな円安トレンドが継続
＜リスク ＞
 日本の財政懸念が再燃することにより長期金
利の上昇を伴った円安が加速するリスク
 トランプ政策による米財政懸念により 、国債価
格、株価、ドルのトリプル安が進むリスク
 米中間選挙後も 、トランプ大統領の 「ドンロー
主義」による諸外国との摩擦が続き 、米国の

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
NOVEMBER CPI
PREVIEW AND ANALYSIS
Complete Montly CPI will take some time to understand
19 December 2025
Key points
• The October monthly CPI was 
flat in the month, broadly as we 
expected but stronger than market 
expectations. For more information see 
“Electrical noise is far from static”.
• The impact on our inflation profile 
was marginal. The December quarter 
CPI remains 0.6%qtr/3.6%yr, while 
the Trimmed Mean edged up from 
0.7%qtr/3.1%yr to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr.
• Our November monthly CPI estimate 
is 0.4% for the month, leaving the 
annual pace unchanged at 3.8%yr.
• The introduction of the complete 
monthly CPI is a significant enhancement 
to our analysis of price formation in 
the Australian economy. However, 
with many series having only a short 
history, it will take time to fully 
understand some of the finer details.
• For now, we will continue to place 
greater emphasis on the quarterly 
measures of core inflation.

### Page 2

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts 
are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
No surprise in the October CPI
The new complete monthly CPI gained 3.8% in the year to 
October, coming in a touch softer than Westpac’s estimate of 
3.9%yr but a bit stronger than the market estimate of 3.6%yr. 
So while the market and other commentators may have been 
surprised by the result it was broadly within our expectations 
and as such has had just a marginal impact on our inflation 
profile.
The October CPI was flat in the month, a touch softer than 
Westpac’s published near-cast of 0.1% on the back of a larger 
than expected decline in electricity (–10.2%) which was only 
partially offset by a smaller than expected decline in auto fuel 
(–0.9%) and an unexpected bump up in garments (+2.6%).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.3% in the month of October, 
the same monthly increase it has seen for the previous two 
months and down from the 0.5%mth increase in July but 
stronger than the 0.2%mth prints from March to June.
This saw the annual pace of the TM increase to 3.3% from 
3.2% in September. This month the TM excluded the annual 
rise in electricity; alongside other large price rises and falls. 
Due to the short history of many series in the monthly CPI, the 
annual pace of monthly TM inflation can only be calculated 
back to April 2025. Before then the ABS notes that the annual 
pace is calculated from the quarterly CPI data.
For more information on the October monthly CPI see 
“Electrical noise is far from static ”.
Monthly CPI points to some momentum
Following the October CPI we have seen some minor revisions 
to the various components in our December quarter CPI 
estimate with the upward revisions, such as transport via auto 
fuel and motor vehicles, being offset by downward revisions 
to recreation, alcohol & tobacco and utilities in particular. This 
has left our December CPI estimate unchanged at 0.6%qtr.   
As our Chief Economist, Luci Ellis, has noted that inflation 
did see a bump in the September quarter and again in the 
month of October. However, the main sources of the surprise 
have had little to do with domestic demand or labour market 
pressures. Rather, a sizeable part of the bump looks to have 
been from administered prices and noise. Since the release of 
the data, the RBA has recognised this issue but at the post 
RBA Board Decision Media Conference in December, the 
Governor communicated that the Bank is more worried about 
upside risks to inflation than first thought.
We agree that some of this momentum will continue into the 
December quarter as highlighted by our upward revision in our 
We will continue to focus on the quarterly prints
Justin Smirk
Senior Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
December quarter TM estimate to 0.8%qtr/3.2%yr from our 
earlier estimate of 0.7%qtr/3.1%yr. 
For the December quarter, price changes that are included in 
the TM go from a low of –0.26% to a high of 1.66%. Readers 
will remember that the TM is calculated from seasonally 
adjusted price changes and not the original estimates so if 
unaffected by regular seasonal price shifts. We estimate 
that the most significant components to be trimmed off 
the top in the quarter are: electricity (2.7%); other services 
for motor vehicles (2.5%); tobacco (2.1%); and automotive 
fuel (1.7%). Trimmed off the bottom are: other non-durable 
household products (–0.39%); garments for women (–0.92%); 
international holiday travel (–0.97%); vegetables (–1.16%) and 
wine (–1.27%).   
However, we do not expect this momentum to continue 
through 2026. As Luci noted in her article “ RBA on hold 
in 2026, risks on both sides ” we expect inflation to keep 
easing, due to the lagged impact of past restrictive policy 
and the unwind of earlier government support measures. We 
believe that many commentators have overlooked these lags. 
Monetary policy typically takes a year or more to fully affect 
jobs and prices, so current outcomes reflect the peak cash rate 
from a year ago and we expect to see further labour market 
softening. Strong hiring across the care economy has masked 
some of that restrictiveness, as has public infrastructure 
spending, but these supports are now fading along with last 
year’s tax-cut boost. We believe that even with the 75bps in  
rate cuts this year, policy remains mildly restrictive, and given 
usual lags, this will keep showing in the data through 2026.
Taking a look at some of the recent larger moves in monthly 
prices can highlight many of the issues Luci raises. Taking the 
seasonally adjusted data, smoothing it with a three month 
average then calculating the change over three months (so we 
have three months on three months percentage change) we 
find at the top end of the list some price increases you would 
not expect to continue at the same pace. 
For example at the top are: lamb & goat (8.1%, highly 
seasonal); accessories (8.1% with jewellery prices surging 
on the back of record high gold prices); audio visual & 
computing services (6.0% due to Netflix subscriptions); beef 
& veal (3.7%, highly seasonal); coffee & tea (3.6% noting 
global coffee prices have passed their peak and are already 
significantly lower); water & sewerage (2.3% IPART decision 
was for a 13.8% increase for Sydney Water in October); 
property rates & charges (2.3% administered prices); and 
electricity (1.2%, reflecting unwinding of cost of living 
rebates). Offsetting some of these increases was falling prices 
for: men’s footwear (–2.0%); vegetables (–2.0% seasonal); 
eggs (–1.5%, reflecting the unwinding of the price spike 
associated with an outbreak of bird flu); and major household 
appliances (–1.2%). 
Core inflation still to moderate through 2026
We are projecting headline inflation to get back to the RBA’s 
target band, but not until later in 2026. We now see core 
inflation back below the midpoint of the 2–3% range at the 
end of the year. We had thought this might be achieved by the 
third quarter but now expect current momentum to continue

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
for a bit longer. However, it is worth noting that our estimate
of the six month annualised pace of the TM is down to 2.4%yr
by June 2026.
Electricity to boost November CPI
Westpac is estimating a 0.4% increase in the November

monthly CPI which will hold the annual pace flat at 3.8%yr.
The seasonally adjusted estimate is 0.5% in the month
suggesting it is a stronger than usual estimate.
Boosting the November estimate is: a 16% surge in electricity
(as the impact of the last round of rebates fades); a 3.2% jump
in gas & other household fuels; and a 2.3% bump in auto fuel
(based on reported pump prices). Partially offsetting these
gains is a –2.6% decline in holiday travel & accommodation

and a –2.9% drop in clothing & footwear.
Take care with the Monthly CPI
Many series in the monthly CPI only date back to early 2024
so seasonal adjustment is somewhat problematic. Some
series did have a longer monthly history coming from the
previously published monthly CPI indicator and it appears
the ABS may have used some of this to seasonally adjust the
data. However, we caution that some of the new data has a
different history to the old data and as such, we expect it is

going to take some time to understand the seasonal behaviour
of the new data.
For example, for male garments there is some difference
with the reported monthly change compared to the old CPI
Indicator series but this appears to be only marginal. However,
for women’s, the difference can be significant with even a
reversal of the sign of change in some recent months. And for
some series with a short history and no monthly indicator data
to compare to some of the adjustment appears to be a little

arbitrary, as you can see in the chart right which compares the
children’s garments index in original and seasonally adjusted
terms. The only adjustment made to the series was in June
2025 when a –3.2% change was adjusted to a flat print. Why?
We are yet to uncover.
You can also see where the ABS has turned to smoothing
processes to seasonally adjust short data series. In the chart
over the seasonally adjusted series looks as if has been
generated by applying a Henderson Trend to the original

children’s footwear series.
As such, we expect it is possible there will be significant
adjustments made to the seasonally adjustment process as
data is collected which could result in significant revisions to
seasonally adjusted series including the Trimmed Mean and
the Weighted Median.

Source PDF: all report pdf/【產業聚焦】算力新瓶頸，光互連新局：ＣＰＯ進入量產驗證期.pdf
Report source: CTBC
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

算力新瓶頸，光互連新局：
CPO 進入量產驗證期
產業聚焦
2026/09/17
• 短期商用確定：1.6T 模組放量、
交換器 CPO 落地
• 中期瓶頸突破：異質封裝良率
與可維護性成焦點
• 長期潛力可期：光學 I/O 仍需
等待 Tape-out 認證
AI叢集擴張，網路效率決定GPU利用率
大型模型訓練由數千顆GPU擴張至數萬甚至更大規模後，系統瓶頸已由單顆晶片效能延伸至Scale-up、
Scale-out與跨資料中心互連，交換器頻寬、傳輸延遲、每位元功耗及故障率若未同步改善，GPU會因等
待資料而閒置，新增資本支出也難以轉化為有效Token產出。市場衡量標準因此由GPU數量，進一步轉向
每瓦、每埠及每個Rack可穩定傳輸的資料量，光通訊價值也隨節點數、網路層數與連線密度同步上升。
雲端客戶的採購重點將更偏向有效算力、網路uptime與總體擁有成本，而非只比較名目GPU規模。
可插拔與 CPO 並行，2026 年迎商用量產拐點
TrendForce估計AI光模組市場由2025年165億美元增至2026年260億美元，800G以上出貨占比逾六成，

短期收入仍由800G／1.6T模組、EML／CW雷射、DSP與FAU領先。另一方面，NVIDIA Spectrum-X
Ethernet Photonics已進入量產，Broadcom Tomahawk 6大量出貨並以Davisson推進102.4T CPO，聯
電亦完成SILITH 1.6T矽光子晶圓首批交付。產業已跨過技術展示階段，但CPO仍會先集中於高Radix交換
器。可插拔、NPO與CPO將依距離、功耗、維修及供應鏈控制需求並行，收入不會在單一年度完成替代，
2026至2027年的主旋律將是分層導入成熟Scale-out技術。
量產與毛利率驗證優先，光I/O保留長線彈性
SEMICON Taiwan 2026把焦點由PIC效能推進至晶圓製程、異質封裝、主動對位、多層測試與現場維修
，顯示競爭核心轉為能否以可控成本大量製造。選股宜先檢查平台與客戶、量產時點、收入占比、良率、
毛利率及資本支出回收，再評估2030年TAM。2028年前後若Optical I/O進入XPU封裝，ASIC設計、光

子Chiplet、先進封裝及Rack整合可望取得更高價值量，但仍須等待Tape-out、標準與系統驗證。台灣供
應鏈的核心觀察值應由合作公告轉為晶圓出貨、封裝一次通過率、Rack部署與毛利率變化。
CPO在AI資料中心的滲透率持續提升

資料來源：TrendForce，2026/03；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

### Page 2

AI萬卡叢集瓶頸：網路效率決定有效算力
Scale-up與Scale-out承擔不同任務
Scale-up連接同一運算域內的GPU／XPU，主要交換梯度、參數與記憶體資料，追求極低延遲
、高雙向頻寬與高Radix，現階段由NVLink及短距離銅互連主導，UALink生態持續推進。
Scale-out則跨Rack或跨叢集擴張節點，重視可擴充性、可靠度與距離，主要採Ethernet或
InfiniBand，已直接帶動800G與1.6T光模組。Scale-across再把不同資料中心連成訓練與推論
Fabric，對長距離光連結、同步與容錯提出更高要求。其中集合通訊對尾端延遲特別敏感，任一
鏈路壅塞或重傳都會同步拖慢運算域；Scale-out則更在意東西向流量、超額訂閱比與故障切換。
光學價值量隨節點、層數與連線密度增加
當Tier-1 Switch由Rack內移至End-of-Row，且單通道由100G升至200G，PCB走線、連接器與
銅纜損耗快速提高，系統必須加入DSP、Retimer及低損耗材料。電光轉換位置因此由面板模組

移向Switch ASIC，形成LPO、NPO與CPO，下一階段再以Optical I/O延伸多Rack Scale-up。
價值鏈不再侷限於光模組，並擴大至ASIC、矽光子PIC、雷射、FAU、先進封裝、測試、系統組
裝與液冷。採購決策也從單一元件規格轉向端到端鏈路預算、每瓦頻寬、可維修性與總持有成本；
能同時提供電、光、封裝與系統驗證資料的供應商將具備更高競爭門檻。
【注意事項】本行所提供之資訊僅供參考用途。本行當盡力提供正確之資訊，所載資料均來自或本諸我們相信可靠之來

AI互連由晶片內部延伸至跨資料中心
資料來源：NVIDIA、Broadcom、Ayar Labs；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

網路層級 範圍 核心要求 光學演進
Scale-up 同一運算域 最低延遲、高雙向頻寬 短銅線、NPO、Optical I/O
Scale-out Rack至叢集 高Radix、可靠度、可擴充 800G／1.6T、Switch CPO
Scale-across 資料中心之間 長距離、同步、容錯 相干光、DWDM

### Page 3

埠速與架構分流：可插拔守量，CPO攻高頻寬
埠速升級：800G維持主流，1.6T進入量產導入期
800G以8×100G/lane為主，供應鏈與客戶驗證相對成熟，1.6T進入8×200G/lane，隨IEEE
P802.3dj與OIF規範推進，已成為2026年AI資料中心升級重點，3.2T預計朝8×400G/lane發展
，仍需突破SerDes、DSP、光源、散熱與面板密度限制，埠速代表單一介面的傳輸能力，並不
直接等同CPO滲透率，短期營收仍會先反映在可插拔模組與關鍵光電元件。不同CSP亦會依交換
器週期、銅纜距離與功耗預算錯開升級，因此同一年度仍將並存多種埠速；供應鏈應以design-
in、認證完成與量產坡度判斷實質貢獻。
架構遷移：光靠近晶片後，價值轉向封裝與系統
LPO移除模組內DSP以降低功耗與延遲，保留可插拔維修性，但通道容忍度較低，NPO把光引擎
置於ASIC鄰近區域，在電氣距離與模組化之間折衷，CPO將光引擎與Switch ASIC整合於同一封
裝，優先解決高Radix交換器的功耗與面板密度，Optical I/O再把光子Chiplet導入XPU封裝，用

於多Rack Scale-up與記憶體解耦。四種方案的量產時點、供應鏈與毛利結構不同，評價不宜混
在同一TAM。投資上最先可驗證的是量產BOM、出貨顆數與良率，封裝內光I/O則需另看Tape-
out、標準與客戶共同設計。

市場價值量，兩者將於不同架構層級長
期共存。供應鏈評估仍應清楚區分既有

數、單通道ASP與生產良率之變化。

架構分工由距離、功耗與維
修成本共同決定
可插拔模組具標準成熟、可快速熱插拔
更換與多供應商生態優勢，1.6T乃至未
來3.2T仍會在主流Scale-out網路層維
持龐大出貨規模；NPO有效縮短電氣距
離，同時保留部分模組化彈性；CPO優
先用於高Radix、超高功耗及面板密度
受限的旗艦級Switch；Optical I/O則鎖
定跨Rack Scale-up運算域與XPU封裝
內部互連。
四條路線更可能長期分工，而非單一路
線全面取代。滲透率斜率將取決於功耗、
系統良率、維修標準與總持有成本。採
購決策還受故障域（Blast Radius）影
響：可插拔可局部更換，CPO若維修恐
需停用整張交換主板，節能紅利極易被

備品儲備與停機損失抵銷，使現場可維
護性成為放量前提。

### Page 6

200G通道縮短銅線距離，CPO降低功耗
電氣損耗降至約4 dB
傳統可插拔架構讓 200Gb/s 訊號由
Switch ASIC 穿越基板封裝、PCB
走線、面板連接器與 Cage 模組後
才進入光收發器。NVIDIA 指出，此
完整電氣路徑的損耗最高可達 22
dB，系統必須在板上加入大量 DSP、
Retimer 與超低損耗板材以維護訊
號完整性，進而推高板層數與散熱
成本。當通道進一步朝 400G/lane
推進時，高頻插損與串擾的設計裕
度已逼近極限。CPO將光引擎移至
Switch ASIC鄰近封裝，將高頻電氣
損耗大幅降至約 4 dB，因而成為
51.2T 至 102.4T 以上高 Radix 交換
器的重要演進方向。未來銅線將退
回封裝基板內或極短距板內傳輸，
跨板與跨機櫃鏈路將逐步提高光連
結占比，封裝內與極短距板內仍將
由電氣互連主導。
200G電氣路徑損耗由22 dB降至約4 dB
資料來源：NVIDIA ；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

6
光路分光結合外置雷射，介
面功耗降至 9W
NVIDIA 資料顯示，傳統 1.6T 可插拔光
模組介面功耗約達 30W（DSP 20W +
EML 10W），而 CPO 架構可將介面功
耗驟降至約 9W（光引擎 7W + 外置雷
射源 2W），此一大幅節能效益，除來
自省去高速 SerDes 驅動長走線的電氣
功耗外，關鍵更在於光路架構的革新：
CPO 採用外置連續波（CW）雷射，透
過矽光子晶片內部分光機制，將每
1.6Tb 頻寬所需的雷射數量由傳統可插
拔的 8 顆 EML 大幅縮減至 2 顆外置光
源，在降低功耗的同時亦將熱源移出交
換器核心。對百萬瓦級 AI Factory 而言，
網路用電下降能直接釋放更多電力與冷

卻空間供 GPU 運算，但系統端也必須
納入外置雷射 ELSFP 壽命、可拆式光纖
連接與現場維修（Field Return）機制，
以確保整體 TCO 真正獲得優化。
光互連功耗由約30W降至約9W
資料來源：NVIDIA；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

400G逼近極限：純矽、TFLN與InP共存
3.2T／204.8T轉向400G
通道
下一代3.2T光模組與204.8T交換器
將朝400G/lane推進，元件須同時
提高調變頻寬、降低VπL與插入損
耗，並維持雷射效率、熱穩定與晶
圓均勻度。隨PAM4接近頻寬極限，
驅動器線性度、接收靈敏度與FEC延
遲也將影響鏈路預算。
Broadcom已展示400G/lane
Taurus DSP搭配400G EML／PD，
先支援低功耗1.6T並建立3.2T基礎；
聯電與SILITH則規劃把純矽MZM延
伸至400G/lane。速度提升不只考
驗單顆元件，也考驗PIC製程、封裝、
光源與測試共同收斂，量產觀察值
應包括頻寬、功耗、良率與客戶驗
證週期。若實驗室性能無法在溫度
循環與晶圓分布下維持，則量產良
率與交期將面臨挑戰。

7
純矽、TFLN與InP依效能及
成熟度分工
純矽MZM具CMOS相容、12吋晶圓與
成熟供應鏈優勢，適合規模化與成本控
制，但高速效率與驅動電壓仍需優化；
TFLN具高頻寬、低損耗與較佳調變效
率，聯電、SILITH及HyperLight正推進
量產平台，封裝與材料均勻度是關鍵；
InP可整合高效率雷射、光放大器
（SOA）與高速調變器，適合主動光元
件，成本、熱與良率門檻較高。
材料平台絕非單一勝者全拿的零和競爭，
最終取決於系統規格、封裝整合成本與
能否導入一線客戶長約供應鏈。實務上
將朝異質整合（Heterogeneous
Integration）演進：由矽基承擔被動
波導與大規模封裝整合，TFLN負責高

速調變，InP則專注提供高效雷射光源
與訊號放大，發揮各自最佳物理特性。
400G/lane材料平台比較
資料來源：Broadcom、UMC、Soitec；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

純矽MZM CMOS相容、12吋規模 效率、驅動電壓
TFLN 高頻寬、低損耗 鍵合、均勻度、封裝
InP 雷射／SOA整合 成本、熱、晶圓良率

SEMICON Taiwan 2026：矽光子轉入量產競賽
矽光子論壇議程由元件效能延伸至完整光子Fabric
SEMICON Taiwan 2026矽光子國際論壇以Die-to-Die至Rack-to-Rack為主題。Cisco、Marvell
與Lightmatter先討論Scale-up、Scale-out及Scale-across架構，說明網路已成為影響XPU利用
率與每瓦Token產出的系統資源；台積電以COUPE說明EIC與PIC透過SoIC整合，聯電把
200G/lane延伸至400G/lane與TFLN，日月光、imec及Soitec則聚焦光電異質整合。議程把設
計、製程與系統放在同一條量產鏈上，反映CPO已進入工程整合階段。
真正稀缺能力轉為可重複製造流程
Lam、Onto、ficonTEC、Advantest與Enli把討論推進至薄膜、折射率、側壁形貌、晶圓篩選、
雙面電光測試、主動對位與翹曲補償。產業問題已由「光能否取代部分銅線」，轉為「晶圓、封
裝、光纖耦合與系統能否在可控成本下大量生產」。2026年後的競爭指標不只Tb/s，也包括

Known Good Die、整體良率、測試時間、外置雷射可靠度與現場維修性。能把多個環節串成穩
定流程的供應商，更可能先取得收入與毛利率。設備商雖可在終端CPO出貨前取得工程線訂單，
但收入可能隨驗收而波動，真正可持續的供應商須累積製程資料庫、縮短對位與測試秒數。

異質封裝拉長製程：KGD與良率相乘成關鍵
EIC、PIC、ASIC與光纖
共同封裝放大失效率
傳統光模組可以獨立測試與更換，
CPO則把Switch ASIC、EIC、PIC、
雷射介面、透鏡、FAU與大尺寸基
板納入同一系統，任何一顆光引擎
或一條纖維通道失效，都可能造成
高價值組件報廢，整體良率也會受
到多個子系統良率相乘影響。當單
一封裝含多顆光引擎時，即使各子
系統良率看似良好，整體良率亦會
因乘數效應而大幅滑落。
ASE的典型流程涵蓋PIC／EIC後製、
晶圓或裸晶鍵合、光學組裝、基板
整合及最終系統測試，橫跨電性、
光學、熱與機械公差，量產管理難
度高於單一元件，製造端還須建立
逐站追溯，把晶圓座標、鍵合批次
與最終通道表現連結。

先測再組與Known Good
Die決定成本結構
量產策略必須在高價值ASIC封裝前剔除
壞Die，並在光引擎組裝前後重複確認
電性與光損耗。晶圓級篩選可降低後段
報廢，主動對位與原位校準決定光纖耦
合效率，翹曲與熱應力控制影響最終一
次通過率；外置雷射與可拆式纖維則關
係到現場維修時間。若測試秒數過長，
良率提升也可能被設備折舊與在製品占
用抵銷；若coverage不足，則會導致
高昂的客退與返工成本。
供應鏈評價應從單顆元件規格，延伸至
良率爬升速度、UPH、返工率、測試成
本與Field Return。只有這些關鍵指標
同步改善，CPO才具備擴張至更多埠數
的經濟性。能以數據閉環把缺陷回饋至
晶圓與組裝製程的廠商，才能在良率爬
升曲線中脫穎而出。

### Page 10

設備商搶先受惠：雙面電光量測與對位前置
晶圓至系統須建立多層電光測試
矽光子雖常採45／65nm等成熟線寬，光學效能卻高度依賴薄膜厚度、折射率、波導側壁、應力
與材料均勻度，製程控制並不簡單。Onto與Enli等方案把薄膜、形貌與光損耗映射前移至晶圓或
裸晶階段，先識別異常區域；ficonTEC強調重組晶圓、裸晶與模組需導入雙面電光量測、原位校
準、翹曲補償及自動對位；Advantest則推進可擴充、標準化的NPO／CPO測試架構。設備投資
因此早於最終出貨放量，量測資料若能與晶圓座標、封裝批次及系統序號連結，便可建立由缺陷
來源至Field Return的追溯閉環，以統計製程控制縮小規格分布。
商業化關鍵指標由標稱頻寬轉向UPH與Field Return
測試覆蓋率不足會把壞Die帶入昂貴封裝，覆蓋過度又會拉長測試秒數並降低UPH，量產必須在
成本與風險間取平衡，應追蹤晶圓與光引擎良率、主動對位精度、整機一次通過率、每顆測試時
間、ELSFP壽命、維修工時與Field Return，相關機會將擴散至薄膜量測、光譜影像、探針與治

具、主動對位、自動化組裝及Burn-in，若標準遲未收斂或客戶採垂直整合，設備需求也可能呈
現專案化與收入波動，設備訂單通常由實驗室、工程線、複製線再走向量產線，與終端營收可能
相隔數季，評價時應區分單機開發、批量複製、耗材治具及維護服務。

晶圓代工升級：由單一PIC邁向先進光電平台
COUPE整合光電晶粒
台積電在SEMICON說明COUPE以
3D鍵合整合EIC與PIC，並透過矽微
透鏡及背面金屬反射鏡優化光路，
成熟的PDK可大幅縮短客戶設計、
模擬與系統驗證時間。平台同時支
援兩種電性架構，未來規劃把調變
器、SOA與光源置於背面，並可形
成最高3.3倍光罩尺寸的光學中介層，
其戰略價值在於將光學與先進封裝
設計規則全面前置。
當COUPE與CoWoS透過系統級技
術共同優化，晶圓代工價值將由單
一PIC製程，大幅延伸至光電
Chiplet整合與先進封裝平台，營收
來源亦涵蓋晶圓製造、異質鍵合與
共同設計服務。量產商用後應持續
追蹤COUPE專案開案數、晶圓層級
KGD良率、封裝一次通過率與客戶
實際部署時程。

光學I/O入XPU封裝：2028年前先看NRE
光子Chiplet延伸Scale-
up距離
光學I/O把電光轉換由交換器旁移入
GPU、XPU或ASIC封裝，透過UCIe
等晶粒介面連接光子Chiplet，可望
在較低pJ/bit下提供數十至逾
100Tb/s的封裝級頻寬，並支援記
憶體解耦與跨機櫃Scale-up，其經
濟性來自以少量光纖取代大量高速
電氣I/O，釋放封裝邊緣與主板走線
資源。
Intel已展示整合式OCI光子晶粒，
Ayar Labs則以TeraPHY推進CW-
WDM光學介面與UCIe連結。相較
Switch CPO，光學I/O更接近運算
晶片與封裝共同設計，單機價值量
與技術門檻都更高。不過光源供應、
封裝熱耦合、記憶體一致性與軟體
拓樸仍須同步驗證。

高價值量仍待標準與良率驗
證
創意與Lightmatter合作導入Passage
CPO，世芯-KY與Ayar Labs合作開發
多Rack Optical I/O方案，緯穎展示整
合TeraPHY、ELSFP、HVDC與液冷的
Compute Tray，顯示台灣供應鏈已進
入系統驗證。短期收入可能先以NRE、
工程服務與小量試產呈現，未必立即轉
為高毛利量產。
然而光學I/O仍需完成Tape-out、光源、
封裝良率、熱設計、UCIe／CW-WDM
互通與客戶量產認證，Optical I/O規模
化收入可望自2028年前後逐步啟動。
投資上應以實際專案、封裝驗證與可追
蹤收入為依據，不能把長期TAM直接視

為近期貢獻，判斷轉折需同時看到
Tape-out成功、封裝一次通過率、平
台軟體支援與第二客戶複製。
Ayar Labs端到端Optical I/O架構
資料來源：Ayar Labs、Wiwynn；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

### Page 14

開放標準決定CPO能否由單一平台擴大採購
Open CPO建立Rack系
統藍圖
Lightmatter與19家公司於2026年8
月在OCP啟動CPO系統架構倡議，
發布約300頁基礎白皮書，目標建立
符合MHS與Open Rack v3的共同藍
圖，使Scale-up域由72顆擴張至
1,024顆以上節點，並支援十萬顆
XPU叢集。共同藍圖可降低每家
CSP自行定義接口的重工。
隨SerDes通道速率逼近448G，銅
線極限傳輸距離驟減至數十公分以
內，促使標準化範圍由單顆光引擎
封裝，進一步擴大至交換器、XPU
運算板、外置光源、光纖理線、高
功率散熱、供電及模組化維修介面。
對台廠供應鏈而言，跨廠商互通性
測試（Interop）與現場可維護性將
成為新一代量產認證的核心門檻。
台灣系統與光元件業者已加入Rack級驗證
資料來源：Wiwynn／Ayar Labs，2026/05；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

14
規格收斂決定第二波滲透率
OIF以3.2T CPO及200G/lane介面推進
光引擎、外置雷射、連接器與散熱；
OCI MSA希望讓不同XPU、Switch與
光學供應商互通；IEEE 802.3dj則建立
200G/lane Ethernet實體層基礎。多套
規格並行有助擴大供應來源，也可能造
成接口分歧與認證重工。對台廠而言，
標準化雖可擴大可服務市場，也會提高
價格透明度與第二來源壓力。
2026至2027年間，關鍵觀察指標聚焦
於各標準規格凍結時程、跨廠商互通性
測試ELSFP外置光源模組壽命及可拆式
光纖連接器之量產驗證。待產業標準全
面收斂後，CPO採購模式方能由封閉的
垂直整合旗艦平台，順利擴展至多供應

商的公有雲市場採購體系。能穩定交付
驗證樣品並累積大量互通數據實績之業
者，方能在規格定案放量後率先取得首
波規模化採購訂單。
四套開放規格的分工與進度
資料來源：OCP、OIF、OCI、IEEE；中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09

### Page 15

台灣供應鏈依營收時點分層，量產證據最關鍵
2026至2027年先看已有平台與出貨實績的環節
台積電以COUPE、SoIC與CoWoS切入光電整合，聯電與SILITH已完成12吋1.6T PIC首批量產交
付，日月光投控／SPIL具備CPO封裝、組裝與測試布局，鴻海與FIT則由光纖互連延伸至Switch
及Rack系統。波若威等被動光元件廠的機會來自FAU、Fiber Shuffle與可拆式連接，但仍須確認
客戶認證及CPO收入占比。這些公司具較明確平台位置，評價重點應落在出貨量、良率、營收占
比與毛利率，晶圓代工與OSAT雖較早受惠前置開發，獲利仍要看量產批次是否持續，系統與光
纖廠較接近終端部署，認列則受機房建置與驗收影響。
2027至2028年再看ASIC、Rack與Optical I/O驗證
創意與Lightmatter等合作推進CPO ASIC，世芯-KY布局跨Rack Scale-up，緯穎展示Optical
I/O Compute Tray，設備與測試商則受惠晶圓篩選、雙面電光量測、主動對位及Burn-in需求。

此層的單案價值量與獲利彈性較高，收入時點也更依賴Tape-out、封裝驗證、客戶認證與標準
收斂。投資排序須區分量產、系統驗證與合作公告。設備商可能由工程線先行受惠，但若規格反
覆或客戶垂直整合，訂單也可能停留在少量專案，須再追蹤同機型複製台數、耗材維護及第二廠
區導入。

### Page 16

產業方向確立，投資回歸出貨、良率與毛利率
量產鏈優先，OpticalI/O保留長線選擇權
部分Switch CPO已進入量產部署，產業整體仍處量產爬坡、良率改善與系統驗證並行，Optical
I/O則仍以NRE、Tape-out及平台驗證為主，2026年最確定的收入仍是800G／1.6T可插拔模組
與關鍵雷射，NVIDIA與Broadcom量產則確認Switch CPO形成第二條成長曲線。台灣晶圓代工、
封裝測試、光纖互連與系統整合具直接位置，設備商可在良率爬升前取得需求。Optical I/O進入
XPU封裝後價值量更高，較可能於2028年前後具規模，應等待Tape-out、封裝與客戶認證。投
資順序可依可見度分成三層：已量產光模組與光源、進入平台部署的Switch CPO，以及仍待
Tape-out的Optical I/O，前兩層重視出貨進度與實質獲利貢獻。
催化劑與風險須同步追蹤
近期催化劑包括Vera Rubin／Spectrum-X出貨、1.6T放量、Tomahawk 6與Davisson部署、

SILITH晶圓交付、COUPE／CoWoS共同設計、OCP首批規格及400G/lane元件驗證。主要風險
則為1.6T／3.2T可插拔延壽、CPO整體良率與外置雷射可靠度低於預期、標準分歧、客戶提高自
製比例，以及估值領先收入。整體產業展望維持正向，選股仍應依序檢查客戶與平台、量產時點、
收入占比、良率、毛利率及資本支出回收。若高速可插拔持續降功耗、CPO維修成本偏高，導入
斜率可能低於預期，反之，規格收斂、第二客戶複製與Field Return下降，將推動導入斜率加速
超越預期。
階段／主題 2026－2028催化劑 應追蹤關鍵指標 主要風險
800G／1.6T AI光模組放量 出貨、ASP、良率 供給增加、價格競爭
Switch CPO Spectrum-X／Davisson部署 Switch數、光引擎良率 維修、Field Return
Foundry／封裝 COUPE、SILITH與OSAT量產 晶圓、一次通過率 客戶集中、資本支出
設備／測試 多層電光測試前置 UPH、秒數、覆蓋率 需求專案化

美系巨頭定錨商用拐點，台廠迎光電整合商機
Switch CPO 率先商用落地，台廠依技術分工迎分層受惠
隨著 AI 叢集朝 1.6T 以上推進，傳統銅線損耗逼近極限，NVIDIA與Broadcom率先推動交換器
CPO進入商用量產，並由Coherent、Lumentum（關鍵 CW 雷射）與Corning（可拆式光纖）鞏
固海外核心供應鏈。在光電異質整合浪潮下，台灣供應鏈具備不可替代的製程樞紐地位，受惠節
奏呈現清晰的階段分流：台積電（COUPE 深度串接 SoIC/CoWoS）、聯電（12 吋 1.6T PIC 晶圓
量產）與日月光投控（EIC/PIC 異質封測）具備最直接的平台量產實績，創意、世芯-KY 與緯穎則
透過結盟海外領先群（Lightmatter、Ayar Labs），率先卡位2028年Optical I/O與機櫃級系統整

中国の成長分野における赤字企業の増加
中国では、 赤字企業が増加している。 とりわけ、 成長分野における増加が顕著である。 この要因の一つは、
し烈な企業間競争による選別である。また、赤字でも潜在力が評価される次世の成長企業も多い。
■成長分野における赤字企業の増加
近年、中国では成長分野における赤字企業の
増加が顕著である。上場企業数に占める赤字企
業の比率（赤字企業比率）は2019年の 9.6％か
ら 2025年に 26.9％へ急上昇している （右上図）。
赤字企業の業種分布をみると、2019～22 年は、
新型コロナウイルス対策として実施された厳格
な外出規制や不動産不況を受けて、飲食・宿泊
業上場企業（以下、上場企業を省略）や不動産
業など非製造業を中心に増加した。 一方で、2022
～25 年には、情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイ
ス、はん用・生産用・業務用機械、電気機械、

化学工業で赤字企業が急増している。これら 4
分野だけで全体の上昇分の約6割を説明できる。
これらの分野は中国製造業の代表的な成長分
野である。実際、この 4 分野の付加価値額の増
加率は、いずれも中国製造業の平均値を上回る。
中国政府が重点的に育成してきたハイテク製造
業も、 これらの分野に多数含まれる。 たとえば、
5G基地局や半導体は、 情報通信機器・電子部品・
デバイスに分類される。ロボットは、はん用・
生産用・業務用機械に含まれる。太陽電池や蓄
電池は電気機械、バイオ医薬品や有機化学素材
は化学工業に属している。
情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス製造業の
近年の動きをみると、2019年頃から世界的に通信規格が4G（LTE）から 5G サービスへの移行
がみられ、2022年 11 月に ChatGPTが公開された。これらが、同分野の成長の追い風となって
いる。 足元では、 一般の消費者や企業がAIサービスを日常的に利用できることが示されたことで、
AI向け端末や半導体、 データセンターの需要が急増している。 こうしたなか、 多くの企業が開発・

製造・販売の強化に注力しており、 同分野の付加価値額は2022年の7,800億元から2025年に9,100
億元へと増加し続けている。その一方で、赤字企業数は同期間に113社から 198社へ増加し、赤
字企業比率は17.2％から 30.1％へと急上昇している（右下図）。
■企業間競争を通じた選別
成長分野でありながら赤字企業が増加している理由の一つは、し烈な企業間競争である。市場
シェアを巡って、設備投資競争や価格競争が過熱している。情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス

JAPAN RESEARCH INSTITUTE
2
製造業についてみると、2022年から 2025年の
期間における固定資産残高の増加率（＋35.4％）
は、付加価値額の増加率（＋16.9％）を大きく
上回る。これは、供給能力が需要を上回るベー
スで拡大していることを示している。また、消
費者物価指数（CPI）は同期間に＋0.4％とほぼ
横ばい、そのうちサービス価格は賃金上昇など
を反映して同＋2.2％上昇している一方で、財
（モノ）の価格は製造業における供給超過によ
り同▲0.7％下落している。情報通信機器価格の
下落幅は▲3.5％と財全体と比べても大きい。
企業間競争を通じた選別が進んだ結果、利益
は競争力の高い少数企業に集中している。情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス製造業の利益総額
に占めるトップ1％企業の利益の割合が2022年の 24.0％から 2025年に 43.3％へと、 大きく上昇
している（右上図）。有力企業は「国内チャンピオン企業」であると同時に「グローバル・チャ

ンピオン企業」としてのポジションを形成しつつある。
■赤字でも潜在力が評価される次代の成長企業
ただし、赤字企業を敗者としてひとくくりにすることはできない。近年、世界的には赤字であ
っても成長性が高い企業に対する投資家の関心が高まっており、中国においても同様な赤字企業
が少なからず存在している。
情報通信機器・電子部品・デバイス製造業に
ついてみると、赤字企業の2 社のうち 1 社は投
資家が高い関心を示している企業である。上海
総合株価指数は、2023年初の 3,089ポイントか
ら2025年末に3,969ポイントへと＋28.5％上昇
した。2025年の赤字企業198社のうち 2023年
時点で上場していなかった 20 社を除くと 178
社となる。この178社のうち 96社（53. 9％）
の当該期間における株価上昇率は、上海総合株
価指数の上昇率を上回る（右下図）。
この相対株価パフォーマンスの良い 96 社の
特徴の一つは、研究開発や無形資産への投資に
積極的である点である。96社の 2022年の R&D

集約度（研究開発投資／売上高）の平均値は
9.6％、無形資産残高の対付加価値額比率も60.5％に達している。無形資産には、コンピュータの
ソフトウェア、特許、著作権、顧客や仕入れ先との関係などが含まれる。これに対し、相対株価
パフォーマンスの悪い赤字企業82 社の R&D 集約度は 6.5％、無形資産残高の対付加価値額比率
は 38.9％にとどまる。
このように、中国の成長分野における赤字企業の増加は顕著であり、その背景には企業間競争
による選別だけでなく、潜在力が評価される次代の成長企業が生まれていることも大きい。

気候変動が高めるアジアの成長停滞リスク
気候変動は、労働生産性や資本蓄積の停滞を通じて ASEAN・インドの成長停滞リスクを高め得る。その
軽減・回避に向け、官民による「適応投資」を促す事業環境と制度の整備が急務である。
■アジアで深刻化する大雨・洪水被害
アジアでは近年、 人的被害を伴う大規模な大雨
や洪水が頻発している（右上表）。記憶に新しい
のは、本年 8月にネパールと中国の国境付近で発
生した土石流であり、本稿執筆時点では死者・行
方不明者が数千人に上る大災害となっている。ま
た、昨年11月にも東南アジア・南アジアで記録
的な豪雨が発生し、 インドネシアとスリランカを
中心に多数の犠牲者が出た。
こうした洪水・大雨のリスクを高める要因の一
つが、気候変動である。気温が上昇すると大気に
含まれる水蒸気量が増えるため、 地球温暖化は豪
雨の激甚化をもたらし得る。 こうしたリスクはア

ジア全体に共通するものだが、 その影響の受け方
は地域によって異なる。なかでもASEAN・イン
ドは、モンスーン（季節風）の影響を強く受け、
雨季に降水が集中する地域が多い。 こうした地域
では、 温暖化によりモンスーンがもたらす大雨をさらに強め、 洪水や土砂災害のリスクを高める。
■気候変動が遠ざける「中所得国の罠」からの脱却
気候変動は、自然災害による人的被害をもたらすのみならず、様々な経路を通じてASEAN・
インド各国の長期的な経済成長力を損なう可能性がある。ADB［2024］は、気候変動がアジア各
国の経済を長期的にどの程度下押しするかについて、定量的な分析を行った。この分析は、IPCC
の「温室効果ガス高排出シナリオ（SSP5-8.5）」に基づき、産業革命以前と比較して2100 年ま
でに地球の平均気温が4.7℃上昇すると仮定したうえで、気候変動がなかった場合の実質GDPと
の差分を推計したものである。推計結果は、気候変動が招く海水面上昇、洪水、熱波などの事象
が、労働生産性の低下や資本蓄積の停滞、消費の

下押しを通じて経済成長に及ぼす長期的な影響
を示している。
結果を見ると、各国・地域とも気候変動がなか
った場合と比べた実質GDPの下押し幅は、2035
年、2050年、2070年と時間の経過とともに拡大
していくことがわかる （右下図）。また、ASEAN・
インドの下押し幅は、2050年時点で▲15.0～▲
8.8％、2070年時点で▲30.2～▲18.1％と、中国
（2050 年：▲4.8％、2070 年：▲10.3％）や中
主任研究員 細井 友洋
(hosoi.tomohiro@jri.co.jp）
